Today’s open-ended creative-AI edition contains 66 detailed guides and 66 additional screened discoveries, including completion of earlier pending guides. New fields cover learned optical holography and generative literature; additional work spans real-footage editing, voice/music, fashion references, interactive installation, CAD and creative electronics. All platforms are represented according to documented support, with unsupported requirements left unknown. All selected software grants were read in full; model weights, paid hosts/services and artwork permissions remain separate. Quality labels reflect six dated evidence checks: a complete guide, activity or stars do not grant Recommended/Ready to try, and only current cited prototype/unfinished-work reasons justify Experimental. These are source reviews; no discovered application was installed or run. The existing weekly pass is complete through yesterday’s 2,484 tools; today’s new/expanded records have current source assessments and the final whole-library coverage gate will be checked. Primary research spans GitHub and all eight configured external ecosystems, with live context for all 100 fields. Noncommercial grants and unresolved license/provenance cases are excluded or watchlisted. Raw unreviewed candidates stay local. Search completion does not mean every returned candidate has been reviewed, nor that discovery is exhaustive. Verified collection: 861/861 repository queries attempted; 861 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 91 bounded/truncated. All 25 model tasks were attempted; 1 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Empty model-source responses: text2text-generation. An empty response does not establish that these creative workflows have no tools. Model-source gap (text-to-image): Source response exceeded the 2 MB collection limit. Primary web research supplements the field; the failed collector response remains a disclosed gap. Separate from successful search queries, 45 watchlist fetches and 5 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Search and review counts

100 creative fields; 861/861 repository queries attempted; 25/25 model-task queries attempted. 51239 distinct source candidates and 1023 model leads. 66 detailed profiles and 66 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 91 bounded repository queries, 1 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

Profiles are based on current primary documentation. Creative quality and performance claims are attributed to the developers unless hands-on testing is explicitly recorded. Unknown hardware requirements stay unknown. Code, model weights, hosted services, and required proprietary hosts can have different terms.

QwenVL for ComfyUI

Documented creative workflow; quality confidence assessed separately · GPL-3.0 · first-profile

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

Docs checked 2026-11-12 · 926 GitHub stars · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Caption reference frames, describe a clip for later editing or accessibility work, and refine image-generation prompts inside an existing node graph.

Evidence checked 2026-11-12; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: The README links a sample QwenVL workflow image and JSON graph and documents image/video node settings. These are first-party examples, not an output-quality benchmark. [1]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: Clone ComfyUI-QwenVL into an existing ComfyUI custom_nodes directory, install requirements with that installation’s Python and restart ComfyUI. GGUF nodes separately require a vision-capable llama-cpp-python build and the matching projector. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
  • Models, services and costs · documented: GPL-3.0 software. Each Qwen or community fine-tune weight/projector license remains separate and must be checked for the exact selected model. Local inference and model downloads; no mandatory hosted model API is documented. [1]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Model descriptions can hallucinate. GGUF vision needs the proper projector and backend handler; context scaling reduces per-frame detail. Seed controls do not establish semantic correctness.

How it uses AI

ComfyUI custom nodes use Qwen vision-language models to describe images and video frames, and text models to expand creative prompts; both Transformers and GGUF backends are offered. [1]

Introduction

ComfyUI custom nodes use Qwen vision-language models to describe images and video frames, and text models to expand creative prompts; both Transformers and GGUF backends are offered. [1]

What it is good for

Caption reference frames, describe a clip for later editing or accessibility work, and refine image-generation prompts inside an existing node graph. [1]

Demo & examples

The README links a sample QwenVL workflow image and JSON graph and documents image/video node settings. These are first-party examples, not an output-quality benchmark. [1]

Install

Clone ComfyUI-QwenVL into an existing ComfyUI custom_nodes directory, install requirements with that installation’s Python and restart ComfyUI. GGUF nodes separately require a vision-capable llama-cpp-python build and the matching projector. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone ComfyUI-QwenVL into an existing ComfyUI custom_nodes directory, install requirements with that installation’s Python and restart ComfyUI. GGUF nodes separately require a vision-capable llama-cpp-python build and the matching projector.
git clone https://github.com/1038lab/ComfyUI-QwenVL.git
pip install -r requirements.txt

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Add a QwenVL node and select a supported model.
  2. Connect an image or a small frame sequence and provide a description or captioning request.
  3. Review the text against the source; adjust frame count, context budget and quantization before longer clips.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
The README suggests 16 GB+ VRAM for FP16, 8 GB+ for balanced 8-bit and lower-memory 4-bit options; these are rough model-dependent guidance, not guaranteed minima. RAM and disk minima are not specified.
Software
Existing ComfyUI and its Python environment; Transformers/PyTorch or separately installed vision-capable llama-cpp-python. SageAttention is optional and GPU-specific.
Platforms
ComfyUI host environment; advanced device controls list auto, CUDA and CPU. The reviewed README does not establish complete macOS/MPS or OS-version support.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: GPL-3.0 software. Each Qwen or community fine-tune weight/projector license remains separate and must be checked for the exact selected model.
  • Commercial: Software use follows GPL-3.0. GPL-3.0 software. Each Qwen or community fine-tune weight/projector license remains separate and must be checked for the exact selected model.
  • Cost: Local inference and model downloads; no mandatory hosted model API is documented.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

Model descriptions can hallucinate. GGUF vision needs the proper projector and backend handler; context scaling reduces per-frame detail. Seed controls do not establish semantic correctness. [1]

AI Coding Video

Documented creative workflow; quality confidence assessed separately · MIT · recently-created

The repository was created on 2026-11-03. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

Docs checked 2026-11-12 · 0 GitHub stars · Release media (2026-11-04) · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Develop explainers, title sequences, visual essays and short vertical motion pieces, starting from reproducible style samples and a reviewed storyboard.

Evidence checked 2026-11-12; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: The bilingual project page links style samples and roughly five-minute case studies with storyboard, source, asset list, time and model-use records. The author distinguishes skill discovery from actually validated rendering in each assistant. [1] [3]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: Clone the repository and use its documented agent integration, preserving the complete skill/sample folder. Actual rendering additionally needs Node 18+, Python 3, Playwright with Chromium, Pillow, NumPy, SciPy and FFmpeg; narration adds a separately configured TTS route. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1] [3]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
  • Models, services and costs · documented: MIT code, original wording and repository-rendered samples. Source footage/generated assets retain the per-sample assets.json terms; models, public voices, fonts and optional renderers remain separate. Coding-agent access and optional speech/image/video services may cost money; local rendering still uses compute. [1] [3]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Not a video-generation model or automatic quality guarantee. Some styles have prompts only. Optional Remotion/Motion Canvas pipelines have independent terms and requirements; this guide focuses on the supplied browser/FFmpeg route.

How it uses AI

A dedicated AI-agent video-making toolkit combines a visual-style library, renderable Canvas/WebGL/SVG/Three.js samples and planning/voice guidance to turn model-written code into films. [1] [3]

Introduction

A dedicated AI-agent video-making toolkit combines a visual-style library, renderable Canvas/WebGL/SVG/Three.js samples and planning/voice guidance to turn model-written code into films. [1] [3]

What it is good for

Develop explainers, title sequences, visual essays and short vertical motion pieces, starting from reproducible style samples and a reviewed storyboard. [1] [3]

Demo & examples

The bilingual project page links style samples and roughly five-minute case studies with storyboard, source, asset list, time and model-use records. The author distinguishes skill discovery from actually validated rendering in each assistant. [1] [3]

Install

Clone the repository and use its documented agent integration, preserving the complete skill/sample folder. Actual rendering additionally needs Node 18+, Python 3, Playwright with Chromium, Pillow, NumPy, SciPy and FFmpeg; narration adds a separately configured TTS route. [1] [3]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone the repository and use its documented agent integration, preserving the complete skill/sample folder. Actual rendering additionally needs Node 18+, Python 3, Playwright with Chromium, Pillow, NumPy, SciPy and FFmpeg; narration adds a separately configured TTS route.
git clone https://github.com/chans-gh/ai-coding-video.git

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3]

  1. Choose a style and adapt its subject, palette and timing to your own content.
  2. Ask a supported coding agent to plan a short scene and adapt its sample rendering code in a separate project.
  3. Render and inspect a representative segment with narration before making the whole film and checking its assets and facts.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
An agent capable of reading the supplied skill files; Node.js 18+, Python 3 with Playwright/Chromium, Pillow, NumPy and SciPy, and FFmpeg for the included rendering route.
Platforms
Local Node/Python/browser rendering; actual assistant execution and cloud-container capabilities vary. Codex skill loading is reported, while its complete film rendering is explicitly not yet tested by the author.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3]

  • Weights: MIT code, original wording and repository-rendered samples. Source footage/generated assets retain the per-sample assets.json terms; models, public voices, fonts and optional renderers remain separate.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT code, original wording and repository-rendered samples. Source footage/generated assets retain the per-sample assets.json terms; models, public voices, fonts and optional renderers remain separate.
  • Cost: Coding-agent access and optional speech/image/video services may cost money; local rendering still uses compute.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3]

Limitations

Not a video-generation model or automatic quality guarantee. Some styles have prompts only. Optional Remotion/Motion Canvas pipelines have independent terms and requirements; this guide focuses on the supplied browser/FFmpeg route. [1] [3]

LenoxVI

Documented creative workflow; quality confidence assessed separately · Apache-2.0 · recently-created

The repository was created on 2026-10-19. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

Docs checked 2026-11-12 · 2 GitHub stars · Release v0.9.1 (2026-11-03) · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Prototype room-specific spoken interactions and distributed voice characters for an interactive sound environment. The source documents the audio routing, rather than a finished exhibition artwork.

Evidence checked 2026-11-12; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: Dashboard screenshots show room, voice and system configuration. Speaker behavior is documented for tested Sonos and WiiM models; there is no independent artistic-result review here. [1]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: On a supported 64-bit Linux host, clone the repository and review its privileged installer before using it. The installer provisions the lenoxvi service and embedded models; complete the dashboard setup, add supported microphones/speakers and configure an actual intelligence provider. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
  • Models, services and costs · documented: Apache-2.0 code. Stock openWakeWord weights are CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 and unsuitable for commercial reuse without different permission; a separately trained wake model is an option. Kokoro, Whisper and external agents/services keep separate terms. Host, microphone nodes and speakers are separate purchases. Embedded speech is local; AI intelligence and optional cloud voices may require paid access. [1]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. WiiM playback interrupts music and does not resume it; Sonos ducking depends on AUDIO_CLIP capability. Nodes do not yet verify the bot TLS certificate. This is a trusted-LAN appliance, not a public-internet installation.

How it uses AI

An open multiroom voice appliance routes microphone input through wake-word detection, neural transcription, an AI agent and speech synthesis to the appropriate room speaker. [1]

Introduction

An open multiroom voice appliance routes microphone input through wake-word detection, neural transcription, an AI agent and speech synthesis to the appropriate room speaker. [1]

What it is good for

Prototype room-specific spoken interactions and distributed voice characters for an interactive sound environment. The source documents the audio routing, rather than a finished exhibition artwork. [1]

Demo & examples

Dashboard screenshots show room, voice and system configuration. Speaker behavior is documented for tested Sonos and WiiM models; there is no independent artistic-result review here. [1]

Install

On a supported 64-bit Linux host, clone the repository and review its privileged installer before using it. The installer provisions the lenoxvi service and embedded models; complete the dashboard setup, add supported microphones/speakers and configure an actual intelligence provider. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. On a supported 64-bit Linux host, clone the repository and review its privileged installer before using it. The installer provisions the lenoxvi service and embedded models; complete the dashboard setup, add supported microphones/speakers and configure an actual intelligence provider.
git clone https://github.com/LenoxSI/lenoxvi.git
cd lenoxvi
sudo bash scripts/install.sh

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Configure rooms, microphone nodes and supported speaker addresses in the local dashboard.
  2. Choose embedded, LocalAI or cloud STT/TTS, then configure a supported AI intelligence endpoint; the initial echo is not an AI conversation.
  3. Test one room’s wake, transcription, reply and music interruption behavior before adding zones.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Compatible ReSpeaker/Waveshare microphone nodes and room speakers or node speakers. Embedded STT/TTS require no GPU; Pi 5 is reported at about 3.5 seconds to a local reply, not a universal latency guarantee. RAM/storage minima are not specified.
Software
Ubuntu 22.04/24.04/26.04, Debian 12/13 or 64-bit Raspberry Pi OS; native service installation, not Docker. Chrome/Edge desktop is required for USB node setup.
Platforms
Linux x86_64 or arm64; Raspberry Pi 5 is a documented example. No macOS or Windows appliance support is stated.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Apache-2.0 code. Stock openWakeWord weights are CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 and unsuitable for commercial reuse without different permission; a separately trained wake model is an option. Kokoro, Whisper and external agents/services keep separate terms.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 code. Stock openWakeWord weights are CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 and unsuitable for commercial reuse without different permission; a separately trained wake model is an option. Kokoro, Whisper and external agents/services keep separate terms.
  • Cost: Host, microphone nodes and speakers are separate purchases. Embedded speech is local; AI intelligence and optional cloud voices may require paid access.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

WiiM playback interrupts music and does not resume it; Sonos ducking depends on AUDIO_CLIP capability. Nodes do not yet verify the bot TLS certificate. This is a trusted-LAN appliance, not a public-internet installation. [1]

How it uses AI

A subtitle-proofreading toolkit gives AI agents explicit bilingual review stages, criteria and standard-library helpers for alignment, change landing and acceptance checks. [1]

Introduction

A subtitle-proofreading toolkit gives AI agents explicit bilingual review stages, criteria and standard-library helpers for alignment, change landing and acceptance checks. [1]

What it is good for

Audit long episodic SRT/ASS projects, preserve terminology decisions and apply reviewed corrections with a traceable old/new-text record. [1]

Demo & examples

The maintainer reports work on two series but ships no episode data or finished subtitles. The complete review chain has only been exercised on one series; counts and helper tests do not establish translation quality. [1]

Install

Clone the repository and use Python 3. The self-contained tools need only the standard library; optional agent skills are separate. Keep the subtitle corpus in its own project and start with the new-series guide and scaffold. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone the repository and use Python 3. The self-contained tools need only the standard library; optional agent skills are separate. Keep the subtitle corpus in its own project and start with the new-series guide and scaffold.
git clone https://github.com/rasashift/subtitle-craft.git
python3 tools/subtitle_probe.py /path/to/subtitles --tsv probe.tsv

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Probe the source subtitles and establish language/version and terminology decisions.
  2. Align bilingual tracks and let a human or AI reviewer assess actual translation differences against source context.
  3. Apply the approved change table with line number and old-text anchors, then verify the output and inspect timings and rendering.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python 3 standard library; SRT/ASS files and an independently available AI assistant for semantic review. Some auxiliary tools require the documented project-directory layout.
Platforms
Python CLI workflow; explicit tested OS/version coverage is not supplied.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: MIT toolkit and documentation. The series footage, subtitle corpora, terminology and finished outputs are not provided; source-media rights and chosen AI-model/service terms remain separate.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT toolkit and documentation. The series footage, subtitle corpora, terminology and finished outputs are not provided; source-media rights and chosen AI-model/service terms remain separate.
  • Cost: Local helper processing plus any chosen AI-assistant access; no bundled inference model or mandatory service.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

How it uses AI

Introduction

A local geospatial workspace couples photogrammetry with COLMAP/OpenSplat Gaussian-scene training and a browser-based 3D viewer. [1]

What it is good for

Build navigable splat environments from overlapping drone photographs, organize georeferenced captures and hand assets to a Blender or game-environment workflow. [1]

  2. For the open source edition, clone the repository, provide accessible Docker, provision the local engines and start the Python launcher. This route does not require account activation; official packaged installers have separate purchase/eligible-account terms.
git clone https://github.com/nsicart/MosaicEarth-Desktop.git
cd MosaicEarth-Desktop
docker info

python3 src/mosaicearth_desktop.py

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  3. Inspect/export to the chosen downstream editor; explicitly opt in if cloud sharing is needed.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
x86-64 source target; compatible NVIDIA CUDA access in Docker enables supported GPU stages, with CPU fallback documented. Numeric RAM, VRAM and total disk minima are not supplied in the README.
Software
Source Linux route specifies x86-64, Python 3.10+, Git, curl, OpenSSL and a running Docker daemon. Engines must be downloaded/built; Windows has a separate source guide.
Platforms
Windows and Linux x86-64 distribution targets; no macOS installer. The browser interface does not add unsupported inference platforms.

How it uses AI

A self-hosted game-asset database includes a model-powered naming/tagging agent and MCP tools for storing, finding and reusing generated media with recipes and lineage. [1]

Introduction

A self-hosted game-asset database includes a model-powered naming/tagging agent and MCP tools for storing, finding and reusing generated media with recipes and lineage. [1]

What it is good for

Organize sprites, textures, sounds and models across game projects and let an assistant retrieve or describe the actual local asset inventory. [1]

Demo & examples

The README describes the asset drawer, project briefs, request/review stages, previews and generation recipes. No independent evidence of semantic tagging accuracy or production use was established. [1]

Install

Run the official Docker image with a persistent /data volume, open localhost:8080 and create the first account. Configure the AI provider in Settings and create an MCP token only for the clients that need it. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Run the official Docker image with a persistent /data volume, open localhost:8080 and create the first account. Configure the AI provider in Settings and create an MCP token only for the clients that need it.
docker run -d --name hoard -p 8080:8080 -v hoard-data:/data ghcr.io/toriath/hoard:latest

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Import a small licensed asset pack and check its names, types and source rights.
  2. Configure Claude or a suitable local OpenAI-compatible vision model for metadata analysis and inspect its proposed captions/tags.
  3. Connect your agent over MCP, search the collection and record generation recipes and project usage.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Docker images for linux/amd64 and linux/arm64; standalone Linux/macOS binaries are also described. Source build requires Go 1.27+ and Node 24+.
Platforms
Self-hosted Linux containers and documented Linux/macOS binaries; browser UI. A local model backend is a separate installation.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: MIT server/UI. AI-model/provider terms and every stored asset license are separate. Pack-license detection is a heuristic and must not be treated as a legal grant.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT server/UI. AI-model/provider terms and every stored asset license are separate. Pack-license detection is a heuristic and must not be treated as a legal grant.
  • Cost: Local storage/compute plus selected Claude API or local inference resources; optional tailnet infrastructure is separate.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

Designed for a private LAN or tailnet, with full-access users rather than per-user asset roles. Some nonimage previews need a browser visit. Semantic metadata can be wrong; visual similarity hashes are not learned semantic search. [1]

werk

The repository was created on 2026-11-11. This is its first full guide in this library, not independent validation of a new release.

Docs checked 2026-11-12 · 0 GitHub stars · Documentation review; installation not tested

Quality assessment: Experimental

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Explore mechanical concepts and CAD starting points for props or kinetic installations, treating output as a design study requiring qualified review.

Evidence checked 2026-11-12; documentation review does not imply hands-on testing.

  • Why Experimental: The current README explicitly calls werk a proof of concept, with failed reviewer calibration and unclosed engineering/safety verification gaps. [1]
  • Open-source software license · verified: The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [2]
  • Creative results · documented: The official conveyor demo shows a STEP-derived 39-solid model and its COVER.md. The reported run used about 3.87 million tokens; many requirements and safety checks remained unresolved. [1]
  • Reproducible setup · documented: On Linux with Hermes Agent 0.23.0+, configure a model provider, install a pinned repository plugin and enable it. Use the doctor/setup flow to create isolated worker profiles and uv pack environments; catalog installation is explicitly not yet assured. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [1]
  • Maintenance and support · unknown: A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
  • Independent use · unknown: Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
  • Models, services and costs · documented: Apache-2.0 plugin; build123d/pack dependencies and separately invoked tools retain their own licenses. Model-provider terms are independent. Potentially millions of model tokens per run; local CAD/storage compute in addition to the configured provider charges. [1]

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Explicit proof of concept marked NOT FOR CONSTRUCTION. Reviewer calibration has not passed, checks can be bound to requirements they cannot detect, and electrical/power/safety domains remain gaps.

How it uses AI

A Hermes Agent plugin uses model builder/reviewer roles and build123d mechanical packs to turn a plain-language machine concept into a CAD package with explicit unclosed verification gaps. [1]

Introduction

A Hermes Agent plugin uses model builder/reviewer roles and build123d mechanical packs to turn a plain-language machine concept into a CAD package with explicit unclosed verification gaps. [1]

What it is good for

Explore mechanical concepts and CAD starting points for props or kinetic installations, treating output as a design study requiring qualified review. [1]

Demo & examples

The official conveyor demo shows a STEP-derived 39-solid model and its COVER.md. The reported run used about 3.87 million tokens; many requirements and safety checks remained unresolved. [1]

Install

On Linux with Hermes Agent 0.23.0+, configure a model provider, install a pinned repository plugin and enable it. Use the doctor/setup flow to create isolated worker profiles and uv pack environments; catalog installation is explicitly not yet assured. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. On Linux with Hermes Agent 0.23.0+, configure a model provider, install a pinned repository plugin and enable it. Use the doctor/setup flow to create isolated worker profiles and uv pack environments; catalog installation is explicitly not yet assured.
hermes plugins install aaddrick/werk --ref <reviewed-40-character-commit>
hermes plugins enable werk
hermes werk doctor

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Create a separate local-disk project from a dimensioned concept or the bundled conveyor brief.
  2. Review dry-run routing and budgets before authorizing actual model calls.
  3. Read package/COVER.md, inspect the CAD and verify every unresolved functional/material/safety requirement before any physical build.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Linux, Hermes Agent >=0.23.0, configured model tiers and uv for environment setup; project storage must be local rather than a network/synced folder.
Platforms
Linux is the stated execution target; Windows/macOS support is not documented.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: Apache-2.0 plugin; build123d/pack dependencies and separately invoked tools retain their own licenses. Model-provider terms are independent.
  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 plugin; build123d/pack dependencies and separately invoked tools retain their own licenses. Model-provider terms are independent.
  • Cost: Potentially millions of model tokens per run; local CAD/storage compute in addition to the configured provider charges.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

Explicit proof of concept marked NOT FOR CONSTRUCTION. Reviewer calibration has not passed, checks can be bound to requirements they cannot detect, and electrical/power/safety domains remain gaps. [1]

Hardware & software

macOS-only Bambu Studio/profile paths. Bambu A1 LAN-only is the stated printer target; P1/X1 compatibility is explicitly untested.

License, model weights & costs

  • Weights: MIT application. Bambu software/printer firmware, model services, fonts and user designs retain separate terms.

How it uses AI

A Python short-video pipeline combines LLM script drafting, speech synthesis, stock/generated visuals and FFmpeg editing, with optional separately invoked upload/publishing stages. [1]

Introduction

A Python short-video pipeline combines LLM script drafting, speech synthesis, stock/generated visuals and FFmpeg editing, with optional separately invoked upload/publishing stages. [1]

What it is good for

Produce and inspect a draft narrated heritage explainer or adapt the channel recipe to your own rights-cleared editorial material. [1]

Demo & examples

The README describes a French-heritage channel and its styling, map scenes and subtitle layout. A pipeline claim of fact-checking against Wikipedia is not independent factual or artistic verification. [1]

Install

Use the canonical repository checkout with Python 3.10–3.12, uv, FFmpeg and the required fonts. Install the default/media dependency groups, configure your own provider settings and run the doctor. The README still contains an older SORADATA clone example; verify paths against this checkout. [1]

Hardware & software

Hardware
Documented default is the 16 GB local GPU door, with a separately offered 12 GB alternate. Ollama qwen3:14b is described around 9.3 GB Q4 and unloaded before keyframes; model downloads can be about 10–22 GB. Whole-stack RAM/disk minima are unspecified.
Software
Node/npm, Docker, SQLite/MinIO services and Ollama; separately configured local GPU renderer and CPU video-finish container. This repository is a host/control panel, not the model engine itself.
Platforms
Homelab Node/Docker workflow; exact tested desktop OS versions are not specified. Do not infer local macOS GPU support from the browser UI.

  3. Render the film, inspect its MP4 and SRT and revise only the changed shots.

A modular browser music studio includes a self-contained device authoring contract and validator expressly designed for people and AI agents to create playable synth and effect plug-ins. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Introduction

A modular browser music studio includes a self-contained device authoring contract and validator expressly designed for people and AI agents to create playable synth and effect plug-ins. [1] [3] [4] [5]

What it is good for

Ask an external coding agent to design a small musical device, validate its interface and connect it to an inspectable live patch; existing synthesis is conventional DSP rather than a bundled neural model. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Demo & examples

The README shows a Keys/Acid Baselines/Speakers patch and links complete device and studio manuals. The developer supplies songs and examples; independent musical-quality and agent-authoring reproduction were not established. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Install

Use the official browser site, or download the repository and serve its plain HTML/CSS/JavaScript with a local HTTP server. The documented browser route has no build step. Device validation separately needs Node.js; no Node version minimum is specified. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Use the official browser site, or download the repository and serve its plain HTML/CSS/JavaScript with a local HTTP server. The documented browser route has no build step. Device validation separately needs Node.js; no Node version minimum is specified.
git clone https://github.com/unboxednl/patchkamer
cd patchkamer
python3 -m http.server 8080
node contract/validate-instrument.mjs devices/synths/Duofone/duofone.patchkamer-device.js

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1] [3] [4] [5]

  1. Open localhost:8080/patchkamer/patchkamer.html in Chrome or Edge, add the repository library and connect an existing instrument to Speakers.
  2. Have your chosen AI agent use the versioned device contract to author a separate .patchkamer-device.js file; validate and inspect its code before loading it.
  3. Audition at a modest level, save the workspace and test device behavior before a performance; export a song with its actual asset and code licenses.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Chrome or Edge, also documented on Android; Python 3 is an optional local static-server route and Node.js runs the contract validator. The Windows Electron build is a separate packaging option.
Platforms
Browser workflow, including Android in the quick start; portable Windows desktop packaging. Specific macOS/Linux native builds and OS minima are not established by this guide.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1] [3] [4] [5]

  • Weights: The complete AGPL-3.0 host grant and separate MIT contract grant were read. Devices are AGPL-3.0-or-later; tunes/presets/samples are CC0, included songs CC-BY-4.0 and the MP3 component LGPL-3.0. AI-client and optional online stem-service terms are separate.
  • Commercial: Software use follows AGPL-3.0. The complete AGPL-3.0 host grant and separate MIT contract grant were read. Devices are AGPL-3.0-or-later; tunes/presets/samples are CC0, included songs CC-BY-4.0 and the MP3 component LGPL-3.0. AI-client and optional online stem-service terms are separate.
  • Cost: Local studio has no mandatory account or subscription; an external AI assistant and optional online stem separation can cost money.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1] [3] [4] [5]

Limitations

A valid device contract is not a listening-quality guarantee. AI-generated plug-ins are executable JavaScript and need review. The optional Stem Splitter sends audio to a separately paid service; the ordinary local modular studio does not require it. [1] [3] [4] [5]

How it uses AI

A Raspberry Pi camera pipeline runs a learned pose model on a Hailo AI accelerator and maps a performer’s hands to MIDI notes and continuous controls. [1]

Introduction

A Raspberry Pi camera pipeline runs a learned pose model on a Hailo AI accelerator and maps a performer’s hands to MIDI notes and continuous controls. [1]

What it is good for

Prototype a body-controlled instrument or interactive sound installation using the current two-dimensional, single-person pose-to-MIDI workflow. [1]

Demo & examples

The README diagrams hand height as note pitch, palm movement as note triggering and left-hand height as CC1. Stereo depth, multi-person operation and the larger audiovisual performance are future phases, not demonstrated current features. [1]

Install

Prepare Raspberry Pi OS and the Hailo Apps infrastructure for AI HAT+ 2, plus python-rtmidi. Place this repository under the documented hailo_apps/python/pipeline_apps tree, activate that infrastructure environment and run its camera application. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Prepare Raspberry Pi OS and the Hailo Apps infrastructure for AI HAT+ 2, plus python-rtmidi. Place this repository under the documented hailo_apps/python/pipeline_apps tree, activate that infrastructure environment and run its camera application.
git clone https://github.com/sebastian-gh/pi-ki-musicvision.git
python pi-ki-musicvision.py --input rpi --use-frame

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Connect the Pi camera and AI HAT+ 2 and select an available MIDI output.
  2. Start the pose view and inspect tracking on one performer before connecting a synth.
  3. Adjust height/range and note triggering in a small patch; account for occlusion and test latency on the actual installation hardware.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Raspberry Pi 5 with 4 or 8 GB RAM is recommended; AI HAT+ 2 with Hailo-10H, a camera and MIDI output/receiver are required by the documented setup. Disk and standalone accelerator-memory minima are not specified.
Software
Raspberry Pi OS, Hailo Apps/GStreamer Python infrastructure and python-rtmidi; a receiving MIDI synthesizer or instrument is separate.
Platforms
Raspberry Pi 5/Hailo deployment; no macOS or Windows inference port is documented.

How it uses AI

A headless Rust composition/rendering engine exposes compose, render, probe and verify operations through an MCP server so AI agents can write musical scores and inspect deterministic audio evidence. [1]

Introduction

A headless Rust composition/rendering engine exposes compose, render, probe and verify operations through an MCP server so AI agents can write musical scores and inspect deterministic audio evidence. [1]

What it is good for

Build a short score, sting or synthetic sound cue under agent direction, then check note timing, loudness, rhythm and spectrograms before listening and editing. [1]

Demo & examples

Four worked demos include a metronome, chord pad, title cue and drum groove. The README publishes controlled signal-check results and known timing/tempo limits; those tests are not independent artistic evaluations. [1]

Install

Install the CLI and optional MCP server with Cargo, or use the source workspace. Configure the MCP executable in your chosen agent client. The documented stack decodes audio and writes WAV/PNG without FFmpeg, a GPU or an audio device. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Install the CLI and optional MCP server with Cargo, or use the source workspace. Configure the MCP executable in your chosen agent client. The documented stack decodes audio and writes WAV/PNG without FFmpeg, a GPU or an audio device.
cargo install cochlea
cargo install cochlea-mcp
cochlea render score.ron --verify

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Start with a demo score and have an AI client alter a small RON score using the documented tools.
  2. Render a short cue, probe its stems and inspect the returned measurements and spectrogram.
  3. Listen separately, revise musical choices and keep verification assertions tied to measurable properties rather than artistic success.

Rust/Cargo for installation; pure Rust decode/render dependencies and an external AI MCP client. No specific Rust version or tested OS matrix is supplied in the reviewed README.
Platforms
Headless Rust CLI/server. macOS, Windows and Linux build parity is not asserted without documented platform evidence.

  • Commercial: Software use follows Apache-2.0. The complete Apache-2.0 software grant was reviewed; MIT is an alternative. The AI assistant and any user audio or score assets are separate, with no neural weight bundled in the renderer.

Introduction

Install

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.

ollama pull qwen3-vl:8b
cd backend
uvicorn app.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 18888
python -m app.worker
cd ../web
npm install
npm run dev

A dedicated AI-agent editing toolkit compiles beat-based JSON plans into FFmpeg edits and uses Apple Vision for person masks/reframing, with a live before/after browser review. [1]

Introduction

A dedicated AI-agent editing toolkit compiles beat-based JSON plans into FFmpeg edits and uses Apple Vision for person masks/reframing, with a live before/after browser review. [1]

What it is good for

Build a supervised beat-synced reel with inspectable looks and handoff to a conventional editor. [1]

Demo & examples

The source links a live-view screenshot and a synthetic four-clip demo. The demo proves neither natural-footage edit quality nor independent grading accuracy. [1]

Install

On macOS 14+, install FFmpeg and Swift tools, clone the source and compile the two documented Apple Vision helpers. Work in a separate project folder with an external agent producing the JSON plan. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. On macOS 14+, install FFmpeg and Swift tools, clone the source and compile the two documented Apple Vision helpers. Work in a separate project folder with an external agent producing the JSON plan.
git clone https://github.com/FridayBull/cutai.git
cd cutai
swiftc -O segment.swift -o segment
swiftc -O people.swift -o people

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Scan your permitted clips and have an agent inspect their contact sheets and beat measurements.
  2. Write a JSON edit plan and render one look frame per shot before the full movie.
  3. Review the live before/after view and export MP4 or FCPXML for manual finishing.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python 3 standard library, FFmpeg, Swift compiler, Apple Vision and VideoToolbox; macOS 14+.
Platforms
macOS 14+; no equivalent Windows or Linux vision implementation is documented.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: MIT project code. Apple frameworks, optional Final Cut/Resolve hosts, external agents and all source music/footage have separate terms.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT project code. Apple frameworks, optional Final Cut/Resolve hosts, external agents and all source music/footage have separate terms.
  • Cost: Local rendering plus any selected AI assistant; proprietary finishing applications are optional.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

AI planning is external; normal filters and beat math are deterministic. People tracking and active-moment choices require review. The README advises closing heavy applications on 8 GB Macs; this is not a universal RAM guarantee. [1]

Clone the repository and run the standard-library Python server on macOS/Linux. Connect your Mastodon account and either a local vision-model server or a selected cloud provider. The source includes optional desktop launchers. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone the repository and run the standard-library Python server on macOS/Linux. Connect your Mastodon account and either a local vision-model server or a selected cloud provider. The source includes optional desktop launchers.
git clone https://github.com/brokengoose/FediCaption.git
cd FediCaption
python3 -m server.app

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Configure a local vision model or your own hosted provider and connect an authorized Mastodon account.
  2. Attach a permitted image or short video and generate a draft description with relevant post context.
  3. Review the full media, edit inaccuracies and listen to Read Aloud before making a separate publishing decision.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python 3 standard library and modern HTML5 browser; local Ollama/LM Studio/llama.cpp or compatible remote vision provider. Video extraction can use browser frames or optional FFmpeg.
Platforms
macOS and Linux are documented; no Windows support claim is established.

License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [2] [1]

  • Weights: MIT client code. Mastodon instance policies, chosen local weights/cloud services and media rights remain separate. Local settings contain credentials and require protected storage.
  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT client code. Mastodon instance policies, chosen local weights/cloud services and media rights remain separate. Local settings contain credentials and require protected storage.
  • Cost: Local compute/model storage or separate hosted API costs; a Mastodon account/instance is independent.

Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [1]

Limitations

Sampled frames can miss actions and models can invent content or OCR. Automatic alt-text and a guard cannot prove accessibility or factual accuracy. Cloud providers receive the selected media; local mode requires actual local inference. [1]

How it uses AI

A music accompaniment system combines learned chord/bass models with real-time tempo tracking and rule-based drums to play along with incoming audio. [1]

Introduction

A music accompaniment system combines learned chord/bass models with real-time tempo tracking and rule-based drums to play along with incoming audio. [1]

Scope: Arrange one to nine permission-cleared photographs into a short static memory zine, using model suggestions only where desired and approving the actual crops and narrative.

A compatible agent can use its workflow, or the local render_book.py helper can create, validate and render a plan. AI analysis sends downscaled EXIF-stripped photos to the selected endpoint; the local renderer alone does not. Analysis JSON is not itself a valid render plan.

How it uses AI

A dedicated AI-agent photo-book workflow combines optional Doubao image understanding and Seedream backgrounds with deterministic Pillow layouts that preserve the original photographs. [1]

Introduction

A dedicated AI-agent photo-book workflow combines optional Doubao image understanding and Seedream backgrounds with deterministic Pillow layouts that preserve the original photographs. [1]

What it is good for

Arrange one to nine permission-cleared photographs into a short static memory zine, using model suggestions only where desired and approving the actual crops and narrative. [1]

Demo & examples

The README describes fixed cover/spread layouts and a default 1600×2133 PNG output. Third-party reference images were removed from the public package; the layout can still render without them. [1]

Install

Clone the complete source, create a Python 3.10+ environment and install requirements. A compatible agent can use its workflow, or the local render_book.py helper can create, validate and render a plan. Configure remote AI separately through private model settings and environment credentials. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone the complete source, create a Python 3.10+ environment and install requirements. A compatible agent can use its workflow, or the local render_book.py helper can create, validate and render a plan. Configure remote AI separately through private model settings and environment credentials.
git clone https://github.com/1NWZ/memory-zine-book.git
cd memory-zine-book
python -m pip install -r requirements.txt
python scripts/render_book.py plan /absolute/photo1.jpg /absolute/photo2.jpg --out work/book-plan.json
python scripts/render_book.py validate work/book-plan.json
python scripts/render_book.py render work/book-plan.json --out work/book-v1

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Prepare an empty work directory and a plan using your own photos.
  2. Review crop, subject and chronology; optional model analysis produces suggestions that must be merged into the documented plan schema.
  3. Validate and render static pages, inspect all original-photo placement and text, and retain the source files and plan as a backup.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
Software
Python 3.10+, Pillow/requirements and an available Chinese font for Chinese type; a separate coding agent or optional Ark model access for AI stages.
Platforms
Python workflow with Windows activation and Unix-shell setup examples; no complete tested OS matrix or local image-model GPU support is asserted.

  • Commercial: Software use follows MIT. MIT original code/documentation. User photographs, fonts, brands and external references retain their own rights; Doubao/Seedream endpoints have separate terms and fees.

Introduction

What it is good for

First project

  3. Check all changed and supposedly untouched regions, temporal continuity, audio and output metadata before handing the result to a production compositor.

Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [1]

Hardware
The author recommends/tests 32 GB+ VRAM, with 96 GB for current above-2K plate workloads; these are workflow-specific reports. Models total about 60 GB; a 150 GB cloud container is an example, not a universal local minimum. System RAM minima are not specified.
Software
ComfyUI 0.43.0+, ComfyUI-LTXVideo, Python dependencies including OpenEXR/OpenColorIO and the specified image/video/segmentation model files.
Platforms
Pack installation is documented on Windows/macOS/Linux, but the neural models require a large NVIDIA backend. A browser on a Mac is not evidence of local MPS inference support.

How it uses AI

A training-free neural inpainting pipeline builds an equirectangular 360° image by projecting and progressively synthesizing overlapping perspective regions. [1]

Introduction

A training-free neural inpainting pipeline builds an equirectangular 360° image by projecting and progressively synthesizing overlapping perspective regions. [1]

What it is good for

Develop panoramic environment references from a scene idea, reviewing horizons, seams and semantic continuity before immersive use. [1]

Demo & examples

The README shows a market-square and Japanese-garden panorama plus an anchored projection diagram. These are author examples, not independent seam, stereo or headset-quality tests. [1]

Install

Clone the source, create its documented Python 3.10 Conda environment and install requirements. Supply access and permission for the actual FLUX-Dev/ControlNet model route, then run world.py with scene, side and sky prompts. [1]

  1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
  2. Clone the source, create its documented Python 3.10 Conda environment and install requirements. Supply access and permission for the actual FLUX-Dev/ControlNet model route, then run world.py with scene, side and sky prompts.
git clone https://github.com/Cedric-Perauer/PanoramaGenInpaint.git
cd PanoramaGenInpaint
conda create -n pano_gen python=3.10
conda activate pano_gen
pip install -r requirements.txt
python world.py --scene_prompt "a garden" --scene_prompt_sides "a garden" --sky_prompt "a clear sky"

First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [1]

  1. Start with one small scene concept and explicitly choose the permitted model resources.
  2. Inspect imgs/pano.png in an equirectangular viewer, checking the wrap seam, sky/ground and repeated subjects.
  3. Use debug masks to understand failures and keep the result as a panoramic reference rather than claiming a reconstructed 3D world.
